================================================================================
LOWPOLY BLUEPRINT LANGUAGE (LBL) v8.14 — MASTER AI SPECIFICATION
For: Low Poly 3D Viewer (Three.js single-file renderer, ghostpoly project)
================================================================================
NOTE — a third input format exists alongside JSON and JS: for a richer,
code-only output that produces an actual Three.js Group (real geometry
code, per-frame tick()/VFX animation, and richer runtime hooks via
userData.sculptRuntime), see Prompt_To_Ts.txt. That is a different
contract entirely — a hand-authored .ts factory module, not this
blueprint DSL — and is documented separately.

This document REPLACES lbl_reference.txt and Low_Poly_v5_3_optimized.txt.
Every technical claim below was verified line-by-line against the actual
renderer source (low_poly_3d.html) on 2026-06-30, with PART 30's v6.6
additions verified against the same file again on 2026-07-01, with PART 31's
v6.7 additions verified again on 2026-07-02, and with PART 32's v7.0
JS-output change verified against the same file again on 2026-07-02, and
with PART 33's v7.1 additions verified on 2026-07-04, and this v7.2 pass's
additions verified again on 2026-07-04, and PARTs 36-45's model
integrity/pose/limb-connectivity fix (LBL_Model_Integrity_And_Pose_Fix_v1.txt)
verified against the same file again on 2026-07-15, and PART 46's v7.5
auto-enhancement geometry pipeline verified against the same file again
on 2026-07-16, and PART 47's v7.6 upgrade-guide audit re-verified
against the same file again on 2026-07-16, and this v7.7 pass's universal
style-lock / measured-proportions / material-mapping / pre-output and
post-render fidelity-gate additions (PARTs 48-53, source: LBL v7.2
Universal Fidelity Improvement Patch) cross-verified against the same
file on 2026-07-17, and PARTs 54-56's v7.8 20-technique CG-pipeline
vocabulary pass, positioning-integrity audit, and contradiction audit —
all three driven BY, and verified directly against, the renderer changes
that shipped alongside them in the same pass — cross-checked against the
same file again on 2026-07-19, and PART 58's render-quality-guarantees
documentation sync (source: lbl_patch_1_quickwins.txt — 4 already-shipped
renderer behaviors that had gone undocumented here) cross-checked against
the same file again on 2026-07-29, and PART 59's documentation sync
(source: lbl_patch_2_medium.txt — 3 more already-shipped renderer
behaviors: image-to-UV painting, reference-silhouette IoU, and
auto-enabled env map for pbr-realistic) cross-checked against the same
file again on 2026-07-29, and PART 61's v7.9 organic sculpted-form
decision framework and multi-ball limb technique (source:
LBL_TECHNIQUES_AND_SOLUTIONS.txt, distilled from direct build experience
rather than a renderer-code change) added 2026-07-30 — it introduces no
new field or renderer behavior, only a decision layer and worked example
over PARTs 10/12/40, which were re-checked against the same file while
writing it, and PART 62's v8.0 modeling/texturing technique vocabulary
audit (source: 3d_modeling_texturing_techniques.txt, a 35-technique
reference list) cross-checked against the same file again on 2026-07-30 —
it likewise introduces no new field or renderer behavior, only
documentation of what the existing renderer already does or doesn't do
against that list, and PART 63's v8.1 multi-part mesh export fix
(source: an external GLB viewer's rig-test-mode field report showing
"1 mesh/group part(s)" for whole-model sdf_blob/CSG-union exports) added
2026-08-31 — a decision-layer caveat on PART 61.2 plus a new PART 63
requiring separate exportable meshes per body part and
isPickable/partName userData fields, no renderer-code change required
since buildScene() already copies each object literal onto its mesh's
userData verbatim. (The "v7.2" in this title is itself a
legacy artifact of the same kind PART 56 hunts for — it stopped tracking
the file's real version a long time before this pass; see PART 56.6.)
Anywhere
the two source documents disagreed with the code, the CODE wins, and the
discrepancy is logged in PART 23 (CHANGELOG / CORRECTIONS) at the end of
this file. Anywhere a documented feature could not be found in the code at
all, it is explicitly marked "ASPIRATIONAL / NOT IMPLEMENTED" rather than
silently dropped or silently kept. PART 64's v8.2 character / mascot /
creature / humanoid sculpt enrichment (source: Character_Sculpt_Enrichment_v1.txt)
appended 2026-09-02 — adds the macro/meso/micro hierarchy, attachment
contract, actionProfile, recessed-eye rules, build-pass discipline, and
anti-shallow quality gates for character subjects. ALL NEW FIELDS
(attachment, actionProfile, surfaceFrequencyBands, collider, destruction,
transformChannels) are OPTIONAL — the current renderer ignores unknown
keys safely, the AI is forced to author a proper component hierarchy
instead of a flat 1-2-part character, and future renderer versions +
the TS factory path can consume them. Applies ONLY when the subject is a
character, mascot, creature, or humanoid — NOT to props, vehicles,
architecture, food, or abstract shapes. PART 65's v8.1 structural
error corrections (source: SYSTEM_DIAGNOSTIC_AND_ARCHITECTURAL_ERROR_
REPORT_THREEJS.txt) appended 2026-09-03 — documents the 4 of 10
anti-patterns from the diagnostic that apply to the JSON/JS blueprint
path (canvas-texture / textureImageColorSpace lifecycle, GLTFExporter
userData hygiene, deprecated r150+ material + color-space tokens,
shared material instance mutation), gives the canonical blueprint-
side compliant shape for each, and adds a 10-item pre-emission
reference checklist. PART 65 reinforces — never replaces — PARTs 3,
7, 8, 15, and 63. The remaining 6 of 10 errors (relative imports,
KeyframeTrack binding, un-normalized quaternions, bone pivot
misalignment, TypeScript contracts, factory entrypoint signatures)
do not apply on the JSON/JS path — see PART 65.3 for the cross-
reference table. Bumped from v8.0 to v8.1. PART 66's v8.2 architectural
feature bridge (source: RDJ_PUBLISHERS_LOW_POLY_3D_SYSTEM_COMPREHENSIVE
_ARCHITECTURAL_IMPROVEMENTS.txt) appended 2026-09-03 — documents the
4 of 10 features that have explicit blueprint-side hooks on the
JSON/JS path (multi-style shading via top-level `shadingMode`,
audio-reactive animation FSM via top-level `animationFsm.states`,
modular socket attachment via top-level `sockets` with the four
canonical Socket_* names, universal rigging standard via the twelve
canonical Bone_* part ids), with the canonical field shapes, the
Socket_* / Bone_* name catalogs, the 6 renderer-side behaviors the
AI doesn't have to author (AO, IK, canvas decals, particle VFX,
spring dynamics, multi-format export), and an 8-item pre-emission
reference checklist. PART 66 reinforces — never replaces — PARTs 5,
28, 30, 64, 65. The remaining 6 features are renderer behavior that
the AI doesn't have to author. Bumped from v8.1 to v8.2.

  v8.8 (2026-09-04) — 3D MODELING TECHNIQUES (source: the maintainer's
    "3D MODELING TECHNIQUES — practical guide for reducing bugs and
    making better models"). New PART 39 mirrors the v1.16 upgrade in
    Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the JSON/JS blueprint path. Six practical
    disciplines: (39.1) catch dumb stuff early — the
    "// ----...---const X" pattern silently comments out the next
    declaration, so always put a blank line between a `//`
    separator and the next declaration, and bake a boot-time
    `validateBlueprint(bp)` helper that runs in the JSON/JS
    validator pipeline and reports floating-part gaps; (39.2)
    build a visual dev loop — runtime knobs and PNG snapshot;
    (39.3) use the right geometry primitive — prefer
    `geometry.kind: "tube"` / `"lathe"` / `"extrude"` over
    hand-rolled box stacks; (39.4) proportions and recognition
    — the silhouette test, reference-first, the "is it
    cheating" check; (39.5) procedural / parametric modeling
    — a `params` object on the blueprint root that drives all
    dimensions / colors / curve points; (39.6) the three
    things that help right now. Adds two new non-blocking
    validators E17 (bbox-overlap gap detector on every
    blueprint `nodes[]` entry sorted by X) and E18
    (draw-call / mesh-count sanity on the assembled scene).
    PART 39 is style-agnostic and applies to every JSON/JS
    blueprint. No new dependency, no new export path, no new
    mandatory blueprint field, no removed / weakened rule.
    Bumped from v8.7 to v8.8.





  v8.14 (2026-09-04) — CS2 PBR MATERIAL PROFILE (OPT-IN CAPABILITY).
    Mirrors the v1.22 update in Prompt_To_Ts.txt and the
    v8.14 update in Prompt_To_Json.txt. New PART 45 (CS2
    PBR material profile) applies the same way to JS
    blueprint generation — the JS path is the
    `eval()`-compiled sibling of the JSON path; it
    accepts the same `meta.cs2Finish` / `meta.cs2Wear`
    keys and runs the same `cs2PbrProfile` + `applyWear`
    helper pipeline. The 45.0.2 "extra capability, not
    a rule" framing is identical on all 4 paths (TS
    prompt + TS image + JSON/JS prompt + JSON/JS image).
    The 4 new MeshPhysicalMaterial properties (45.5) are
    wired on top of the existing PART 3 + PART 7 #7 PBR
    Extensions plumbing; nothing existing is touched. The
    8 new MAT_DB entries (`brushedsteel`, `gunmetal`,
    `damascussteel`, `anodizedblue`, `anodizedred`,
    `anodizedgold`, `casehardened`, `polishedmetal`) are
    accessible via the existing PART 5 `material:
    '<name>'` field — no syntax change. The 5 new
    canonical helpers are re-exported on
    `window.lblSpec.helpers` alongside the PART 35 +
    PART 39 + PART 40 + PART 41 + PART 42 + PART 43 +
    PART 44 bundle. The `materialVariants` helper from
    PART 41.6 is EXTENDED with 1 new family
    (cs2-finish); the existing 4+2 = 6 families stay
    exactly as they were. No new dependency, no new
    export path, no new mandatory blueprint field, no
    removed / weakened rule. (45.13) Component Recipe Database for weapons (knife blade types + handle construction, pistol components, rifle components, boolean operations, edge treatment) preserved verbatim from the source notebook as a content guide; (45.14) bilingual EN/VI Vocabulary Glossary (5 core terms + 5 spatial modifiers + the 4-way damage-type × PBR-map rubric) preserved as a reference resource for the AI; (45.15) updated cross-references. Bumped from v8.13 to v8.14.

  v8.13 (2026-09-04) — PROFESSIONAL PIPELINE (GLB-FIRST + BVH + LOOK-DEV
    LIGHTS + CODING RULES).
    Source: the maintainer's "Three.js / procedural 3D character
    and asset systems engineer" brief (2026-08). New PART 44
    mirrors the v1.21 upgrade in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the
    JSON/JS blueprint path. The blueprint path doesn't have
    a `.ts` factory file in the same way the TS path does, so
    the 5-task viewer adapter (44.7) maps to the JSON/JS
    equivalent: the blueprint's `meta` carries the same
    fields the TS factory's `userData.sculptRuntime` carries
    on the TS path. The 4 new pieces map to the JSON/JS path
    as: (44.1) three-mesh-bvh — the blueprint declares
    `meta.bvhIndex: true` (or `meta.useBVH: true`) so the
    renderer knows to build the BVH index at load time; the
    `createBVHIndex` helper (44.10) handles the same on both
    paths; (44.2) look-dev lights per model — the blueprint
    declares `meta.lookDevLights = { key, fill, rim,
    environmentMap }`; the renderer's E29 validator checks
    the structure; (44.3) material hierarchy with bone + magic
    — the blueprint uses `material.kind: 'bone' | 'magic'` in
    the existing per-region `material` field; the
    `materialVariants` helper from PART 41 is EXTENDED with
    the 2 new families; (44.4) fitted clothing/armor layers —
    the blueprint's existing `nodes[]` entries support the
    fit-over-body pattern via the `factory` field; the
    per-node `material.kind` selects the family; the
    per-node `userData.fitOffset` and `userData.fitScale`
    fields declare the offset + scale-up values; (44.5)
    animation clip baking — the blueprint declares
    `meta.animations: { idle, walk, attack, hit }` and
    `meta.animationSource: 'mocap' | 'hand-key' | 'procedural'`;
    (44.6) multi-view validation renders — the blueprint's
    `meta.validationGrid` carries the 18-render grid; the
    JSON/JS path can also declare
    `meta.validationClips: ['idle', 'walk', 'attack', 'hit']`;
    (44.7) the 5-task adapter maps to the JSON/JS blueprint
    as: load via `lblRouteFiles` (PART 36) + extract sockets
    from `meta.sockets` (PART 33.1[7]) + wire animation mixer
    via `meta.animations` + wire look-dev lights via
    `meta.lookDevLights` + wire validation via the renderer's
    existing E1-E27 (now E1-E29) validators; (44.8) the 6
    practical coding rules apply to the JSON/JS path the
    same way they apply to the TS path; the JSON/JS path
    doesn't have `//` comment separators (it's JSON, not
    TypeScript), so 44.8.1 and 44.8.2 don't apply; the other
    4 rules (prefer the correct primitive, drive from a
    config object, add tweakable parameters, include
    multi-view validation renders) apply via the blueprint's
    `meta` field; (44.9) the same 2 new non-blocking
    validators E28 + E29 with the JSON/JS-specific read paths
    for `meta.bvhIndex` / `meta.useBVH` + `meta.lookDevLights`;
    (44.10) the same 2 new canonical helpers
    `createBVHIndex(root, opts)` and `lookDevLights(opts)` re-
    exported on window.lblSpec.helpers. PART 44 is style-
    agnostic and applies to every JSON/JS blueprint. No new
    dependency, no new export path, no new mandatory
    blueprint field, no removed / weakened rule. Bumped from
    v8.12 to v8.13.
  v8.12 (2026-09-04) — REFINEMENT + STYLE LOCKING + VARIANT RANKING.
    Source: the maintainer's "Techniques to Update Your System
    for Better 3D Models" (2026-08), which distills 7
    categories. New PART 43 mirrors the v1.20 upgrade in
    Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the JSON/JS blueprint path. The 3
    NEW categories (refinement, style & consistency, variant
    ranking) map to the same 7 sub-sections: (43.1) geometry
    refinement techniques — (43.1.1) normal-guided refinement
    (the blueprint declares the pass count on
    `meta.normalRefinementPasses`; the JSON/JS path runs the
    same per-vertex shift via the existing part-library
    primitives from PART 39.5.1), (43.1.2) view-by-view
    iterative refinement (the blueprint declares the pass
    count on `meta.viewRefinementPasses` and the per-view
    refinement log on `meta.viewRefinementLog`; consumes
    the PART 40.1.3 multi-view image support), (43.1.3)
    detail enhancement networks (the blueprint declares the
    network used on `meta.detailEnhancer`; for JSON/JS the
    network is usually a procedural noise source since the
    blueprint path doesn't have access to the AI's authored
    networks), and (43.1.4) segmentation + part-aware
    generation (the blueprint declares the part names on
    `meta.segmentation`; EXTENDS the PART 41.1 detail
    inventory); (43.2) style & consistency controls —
    (43.2.1) strong style locking (the blueprint declares
    the style on `meta.styleLock`; one of 'pure-low-poly' |
    'stylized' | 'semi-realistic' | 'realistic' | 'voxel' |
    'hand-painted' | 'toon-cel' | 'retro-psx' | 'hologram' |
    'wireframe'; without the lock, the model is still style-
    agnostic per PART 38.22), (43.2.2) consistent polygon
    density and edge flow across model families (the
    blueprint declares `meta.familyDensity` +
    `meta.familyEdgeFlow` for N-model scenes), (43.2.3)
    silhouette-first optimization (the blueprint declares
    `meta.silhouettePriority` — default
    ['front', '3/4-front', 'side', '3/4-back', 'back',
    'top']), and (43.2.4) hard-surface vs organic topology
    (the blueprint declares `meta.topologyPreference` —
    'hard-surface' | 'organic' | 'mixed' | 'auto-detect');
    (43.3) variant generation + automatic ranking — (43.3.1)
    generate N variants (default N=3, override via
    `meta.variantCount`; the variants are separate top-level
    keys `variants: [bp1, bp2, bp3]`), (43.3.2) rank by
    quality metrics (the new `rankVariants` helper from 43.6
    scores each variant on the E1-E27 warnings), and
    (43.3.3) user feedback loop (EXTENDS PART 40.7.4 with
    `meta.favoriteSeed` + `meta.averageRating`); (43.4) what
    PART 43 reinforces from PART 40-42; (43.5) renderer-
    side runtime contract — adds E26 (style-lock compliance)
    and E27 (multi-view consistency) to the existing 25,
    with the JSON/JS-specific read paths for `meta.styleLock`
    + `meta.referenceViews` + `meta.silhouettePriority` +
    `meta.viewRefinementPasses`; (43.6) the same 2 new
    canonical helpers `viewRefinePass` and `rankVariants`
    re-exported on window.lblSpec.helpers for the rare case
    where a JSON/JS blueprint wants the same refinement-plan
    / variant-ranking behavior the TS factory gets. PART 43
    is style-agnostic and applies to every JSON/JS
    blueprint. No new dependency, no new export path, no
    new mandatory blueprint field, no removed / weakened
    rule. Bumped from v8.11 to v8.12.
  v8.11 (2026-09-04) — CHARACTER PRODUCTION DISCIPLINE.
    Source: the maintainer's "3D Character Production Plan —
    Lead 3D Technical Director" (2026-08). New PART 42 mirrors
    the v1.19 upgrade in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the JSON/JS
    blueprint path. The 7 production techniques apply to the
    blueprint's per-region `material.kind` + `material.maps`
    fields; the 60/25/15 polygon distribution rule applies to
    the blueprint's `meta.polyDistribution` field; the 11-step
    pipeline applies to the blueprint's `meta.productionStep`
    (current step 1-11) + the 7 deformation fixes apply to
    `meta.deformationFixes`; the 5-LOD chain applies to
    `meta.lodChain` (the existing PART 40.6.3 / PART 41 LOD
    support is the implementation hook); the 85-bone skeleton
    reference applies to the JSON/JS path's
    `meta.sculptRuntime.bones` analog; the 30+ blend shapes
    apply to `meta.sculptRuntime.blendShapes`; the half-budget
    cut list applies to `meta.halfBudgetCutList`; the film-
    quality upgrade list applies to `meta.filmQualityItems` +
    `meta.targetQuality` ("game" / "film" / "mobile"). The
    renderer's E24 production-completeness validator (42.9)
    checks that the factory's `meta` includes the 11 steps
    AND the 7 deformation fixes AND the 10 film-quality items
    (if `meta.targetQuality: "film"`) for character subjects;
    non-character subjects are exempt. The renderer's E25
    polygon-distribution validator (42.9) checks the actual
    silhouette/deformation/detail split (computed by
    `polyDistribution(root)`) against the declared
    `meta.polyDistribution` percentages within ±10% per axis.
    Both non-blocking. Adds 2 new canonical helpers
    `polyDistribution(root)` (returns the structured
    silhouette/deformation/detail split based on the actual
    castShadow / isSkinnedMesh / neither flags) and
    `productionChecklist(meta)` (returns the structured
    { steps, deformationFixes, lodChain, halfBudgetCutList,
    filmQualityItems, targetQuality, completeness } shape
    with the 0-1 completeness score) re-exported on
    window.lblSpec.helpers for the rare case where a JSON/JS
    blueprint wants the same audit / split-computation the TS
    factory gets. PART 42 is style-agnostic and applies to
    every JSON/JS blueprint. No new dependency, no new export
    path, no new mandatory blueprint field, no removed /
    weakened rule. Bumped from v8.10 to v8.11.
  v8.10 (2026-09-04) — HIGH-DETAIL GENERATION.
    Source: the maintainer's "System High-Detail Update
    Plan" (2026-08), which targets the gap between current
    ~20 KB models and higher-end >60 KB models. New PART 41
    mirrors the v1.18 upgrade in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the
    JSON/JS blueprint path. The same central principle
    applies: PREFER GEOMETRIC DENSITY OVER CODE BREVITY.
    (41.1) detail inventory before any geometry — the
    blueprint publishes `meta.detailInventory` with 4
    categories (identity / secondary / tertiary / material
    zones) before any `nodes[]` entry; the renderer's E22
    validator checks the actual mesh count against the
    inventory totals; (41.2) the 5-stage pipeline — the
    blueprint's authoring is split into the same 5 stages
    (Blockout → Structure → Density → Surface → Polish);
    each stage adds to the previous stage's `nodes[]` array
    and is independently re-runnable; (41.3) force higher
    component count — the same subject-class floors as the
    TS path (character = 200, vehicle = 150, weapon = 80,
    prop = 40, architecture = 100); the blueprint declares
    `meta.minMeshes` and `meta.subjectClass` so the
    renderer's E23 validator knows the floor; (41.4) the
    same 6 geometry vocabulary patterns, applied to the
    JSON/JS `geometry.kind` field (the
    `geometry.kind: "instanced"` for rivets, the
    `geometry.kind: "lathe"` for rings, the manual box
    stacks for layered armor); (41.5) silhouette &
    proportion pass — the same 4 checks; (41.6) material
    system upgrade — the same 4 families × 4 variants; the
    blueprint declares `material.variant` instead of
    `material.color` for the variant-aware materials; the
    `materialVariants` helper (41.10) is the canonical way
    to look up the right variant when the AI is uncertain;
    (41.7) hierarchy & runtime quality — the same 4
    tightening rules; the blueprint must declare at least
    2 of the 4 canonical Socket_* attachment points and
    a complete `meta.sculptRuntime` analog (using
    `meta.nodes` instead of `userData.sculptRuntime.nodes`);
    (41.8) the 7 prompt rules; (41.9) renderer-side
    runtime contract — adds E22 + E23 to the existing 21;
    (41.10) the same 2 new helpers (`materialVariants`,
    `detailInventory`) re-exported on window.lblSpec.helpers
    for the rare case where a JSON/JS blueprint wants the
    same variant-lookup / inventory-shape behavior the TS
    factory gets. PART 41 is style-agnostic and applies to
    every JSON/JS blueprint. No new dependency, no new
    export path, no new mandatory blueprint field, no
    removed / weakened rule. Bumped from v8.9 to v8.10.
  v8.9 (2026-09-04) — BETTER MODELS THROUGH BETTER GENERATION.
    Source: the maintainer's "System Update Details for Better
    TypeScript Low-Poly 3D Models" (2026-08), which distills
    8 categories of improvements the AI must apply when
    generating a JSON/JS blueprint. New PART 40 mirrors the
    v1.17 upgrade in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the JSON/JS
    blueprint path. The 8 categories map to the same 11
    sub-sections: (40.0) central principle — production-
    grade generation pipeline, not one-shot prompt-to-
    geometry; (40.1) better prompt & input handling —
    structured brief (Subject + Material + Style +
    Technical Constraints), image preprocessing, multi-view
    support, negative prompts as hard constraints; (40.2)
    multi-stage pipeline — 4 stages (coarse base mesh,
    geometry refinement, texture / material, final cleanup
    + export) with the same Stage 2 / Stage 3 boundary as
    the TS path; (40.3) mesh quality & topology — automatic
    cleanup (weld duplicate vertices, remove zero-area
    triangles, remove unused vertices, remove degenerate
    edges), smart low-poly mode with targetTris, forced
    watertight; (40.4) structured output — each `nodes[]`
    entry has a `factory` field that names the part-library
    primitive it uses (PART 39.5.1), and the blueprint
    exposes a `params` config object on the root (the
    JSON/JS analog of the TS config object from 40.4.2 /
    39.5.2) that drives all dimensions, colors, curve
    points; (40.5) texture & material system — full PBR
    texture sets with style-specific texturing (low /
    mid / high density), separate texture refinement stage,
    automatic UV handling; (40.6) parametric & procedural
    techniques — the params-object pattern, seed-based
    controlled variation via the `seed` meta field, LOD
    support via the existing `meta.lod` field, prefer
    algorithmic geometry (1 factory + 1 InstancedMesh of N
    instances); (40.7) post-processing, validation &
    feedback — 3 new non-blocking validators E19 (PBR + UV
    completeness), E20 (hard-constraint compliance / thin
    spike detector), E21 (target triangle budget) + the
    same mesh repair pipeline as the TS path + an optional
    user-rating feedback loop; (40.8) scene / composition
    — consistent style and density, hierarchical scene
    building, procedural placement and variation; (40.9)
    renderer-side runtime contract — adds the same 3
    validators as the TS path, with the JSON/JS-specific
    read paths for `meta.style`, `meta.textureDensity`,
    `meta.targetTris`, and `meta.negativePrompts`; (40.10)
    canonical helpers — 3 new helpers re-exported on
    `window.lblSpec.helpers`: `pbrMaterial`, `seededRandom`,
    `triCountSummary` (the JSON/JS path can call them the
    same way the TS path does, but most JSON/JS blueprints
    won't call them directly — they exist for the rare
    case where a JSON/JS blueprint wants the same
    PBR-with-full-texture-set / seeded-RNG / tri-count
    summary behavior the TS factory gets). PART 40 is
    style-agnostic and applies to every JSON/JS blueprint.
    No new dependency, no new export path, no new
    mandatory blueprint field, no removed / weakened rule.
    Bumped from v8.8 to v8.9.
  v8.7 (2026-09-04) — CHARACTER PIPELINE UPGRADE.
    New PART 38 mirrors the v1.15 upgrade in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for
    the JSON/JS blueprint path. The character output path is
    upgraded from primitive-assembly to a continuous-topology +
    deformation-aware + GLB-first pipeline. 21 sub-sections
    covering the central principle, the 10-point diagnosis, the
    2-layer architecture, the 14-stage production pipeline + 7
    quality gates, the character specification schema (identity /
    proportions / silhouette / face / costume / palette / rig /
    animation / budget), the 4 continuous-mesh-generation
    approaches (parametric / lofted / implicit / hybrid), the
    topology techniques + edge-loop locations, the polygon +
    vertex budgeting, the facial modeling system + goblin
    identity features, the clothing + armor + props system, the
    UV + texture system, the real skeletal rigging standard, the
    animation techniques, the GLB-first architecture, the 3
    tooling options, the validation system (E13-E16 are NEW
    character-specific non-blocking validators; E1-E12 are
    unchanged), the multi-view + silhouette review, the LOD +
    performance strategy, the 9-phase implementation roadmap,
    the 10-step priority order, and the final design principle.
    PART 38 is OPT-IN for character / mascot / creature / humanoid
    subjects and INFORMATIONAL otherwise. The existing PART 30
    pivot hierarchy remains valid for non-character subjects and
    for jointed-only character poses. PART 38 reinforces — never
    replaces — PARTs 1-66. Bumped from v8.6 to v8.7.

  v8.4 (2026-09-03) — TRIANGLE-FIRST GEOMETRY (TRIS) (source:
    WORKING_WITH_TRIANGLES guide attached by the maintainer).
    New PART 35 documents the 5 methods of working with
    triangles in Three.js, adapted for the JSON/JS blueprint
    DSL: (1) the new `mergeGeometries: true` part-level flag
    that collapses multi-shape parts into a single
    BufferGeometry, (2) the `customGeometry.type` extension
    (now accepts both "nonIndexed" and "indexed" raw triangle
    buffers), (3) low-poly flat shading + segment optimization
    with a 50,000-tri soft cap and the new top-level
    `meta.trisBudget` / `meta.trisBudgetTolerance` declarative
    soft-cap, (4) the triangle count calculator + per-geometry
    metrics inspector (PART 65.2's `inspectModelTriangles`
    EXTENDED with 4 additive fields: totalTriangles,
    totalVertices, drawCalls, geometries[].indexed — original
    5 fields preserved for back-compat), and (5) the correct
    attachTo + updateMatrixWorld order for merged triangle
    meshes. Adds the 6-item pre-emission checklist (items
    17-22 — APPENDED to PART 65.3's 16-item list) and a
    runtime contract describing the renderer's new E12
    validator + the `🔺 Tris` topbar button (enabled for every
    loaded model) + the `window.lblSpec.helpers` re-exports
    for any .ts factory that wants to call the underlying

    — PART 1, 2, 4, 22, 30, 32, 33, 34, 64, 65, 66. Bumped
